### This Newsroom Wants You to Be Part of the Journey Before It Even Starts
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-06 10:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/this-newsroom-wants-you-to-be-part-of-the-journey-before-it-even-starts/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

My happiest times in journalism have been when I have built something new. For my latest venture, I want to help bring investigative journalism on climate and environmental issues to a much larger audience than it has reached in the past. To do so, I’m starting a brand-new newsroom focused on the environmental and climate crisis, and I’m taking my audience along for the ride. I’ll be documenting every facet of the process with an “open kitchen” approach of radical transparency, starting with a 100-day video countdown chronicling all the steps involved with starting a news organization from the ground up. This effort combines two of the things I’m most passionate about: I love digging up stories that other people don’t want to see published, and I love creating opportunities for other journalists. I’ve been a journalist for most of my life. I started working at age 14 for the local paper in my rural German town, turned to doing investigative stories in my early 20s, and later studied investigative journalism at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism in New York.

### America’s Shrinking Local Newsrooms, Through a Photographer’s Lens
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-02 12:30 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/americas-shrinking-local-newsrooms-photographer-ann-hermes-local-news-news-deserts-rural-america/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For many journalists, the sight of empty desks is a sadly familiar one. My first glimpse of a gutted newsroom facing further cutbacks sparked a documentary photography project that has so far taken me to more than 50 newsrooms across the United States over the past eight years. It began when my mother, a loyal subscriber to our local metro in southern Illinois, told me a decade ago how disappointed she was in the newspaper. She complained that the local coverage was lacking, and the paper seemed to be thinner. Already several years into my photojournalism career, I was unsurprised by her remarks — I had been witnessing the decline of print media since graduation from journalism school. Still, my curiosity was piqued, and I arranged a visit to the paper to see for myself. Cue the scene of a barren newsroom. Not only were there empty desks, but also an expanse of exposed carpet with indentations showing where desks had once long stood. While there has been a great deal of reporting on the issues plaguing local journalism, I hadn’t seen the story told visually.

### Seven Takeaways on Convenience and News
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-25 14:53 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gabe-bullard-against-convenience-book-technology-friction-news-online-social-media/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

"Against Convenience: Embracing Friction in an Age of Endless Ease" was released on Sept.15, 2026, by Hanover Square Press, an imprint of HarperCollins Publishers. Courtesy of Hanover Square Press From the earliest pitch, I knew that I wanted to cover the news industry in my book, “Against Convenience.” The book looks at the technology around us and asks whether it’s really improving our lives. Is delivery food worth the higher price? Is streaming music better than collecting CDs or MP3s? News embodies the paradox of modern convenience: We have access to more information than ever before, but misinformation and partisanship are rampant and the purveyors of reliable information are struggling to stay solvent. Plus, I figured if I was going to criticize other fields — tech, entertainment, appliance manufacturing — I couldn’t avoid my own. I don’t know how interested the average nonjournalist is in the day-to-day operations of the news business, so I tried to look in the mirror more than gaze at my navel.

### The YouTuber Shaking Up Brazil’s Powerful Sports Media
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-21 17:04 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/caze-tv-casimiro-miguel-fifa-world-cup-twitch-youtube-streamer-sports-journalism-tv-globo-brazil/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For more than half a century, Brazil’s TV Globo was the undisputed home of soccer in a nation obsessed with it. That dominance is now being tested by Casimiro Miguel, known as “Cazé” — a 32-year-old journalism school dropout who has built a massive online following and shaken up the Brazilian sports broadcasting landscape. Powered by features like live chat and extended pre- and post-game analysis, CazéTV has accomplished what traditional television networks dream of: pulling in new viewers and keeping them engaged for hours. FIFA, soccer’s global governing body, describes CazéTV “the top digital sports platform in Brazil” and calls it “central to its content strategy.” That growth has come with controversy and accusations of misogyny and a lack of diversity. Brazilian regulators are investigating suspected predatory advertising practices by CazéTV. Still, Nicolas Duprat, a Brazilian sports media researcher who has studied CazéTV for years, credits the channel with helping democratize access for fans who can’t afford stadium tickets or a TV Globo subscription.

URL: https://niemanreports.org/the-continent-south-africa-kenya-nairobi-journalism-africa-industry-friendships-collaboration-magazines-sipho-kings-christine-mungai/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The opening sentence of my first message to Christine Mungai ended with the words: “fellow continent person.” It was August 2017, and we were the two Africans in that year’s cohort of Nieman journalism fellows at Harvard University. Although our home countries were about 4,000 kilometers (approximately 2,500 miles) apart in Africa, being from here means sharing a shorthand understanding of the world with your fellow Africans. That shorthand doesn’t have many structures to support it, however. Nine years after our first meeting, Christine and I are now working together to build one such structure: The Continent, a 30-page digital newspaper that aims to create a shared reality for Africans to learn about their communities, one another, and our continent. In a world that seeks to segregate us, this pan-African platform lets people literally read from the same page. As one reader said, the weekly provides “biting criticisms of power in contemporary Africa, with love as the engine.

### Focusing on Fans Captures the Magic of the World Cup
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-08-10 16:42 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/world-cup-fans-yutao-chen-film-photography-2026-new-york-new-jersey-spain-haiti-argentina-england/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

Photographer Yutao Chen first developed an interest in soccer during his childhood. As a lifelong follower of the sport, he seized the opportunity to cover the 2026 FIFA World Cup, traveling across the United States to document fan reactions throughout the tournament. He undertook the project independently, financing it himself rather than on assignment. Chen chose to work in film over digital, he explained, because he wanted these images to be imperfect, personal artifacts, shaped by his own perception rather than an exact record of events. This conversation has been edited and condensed for clarity. The first World Cup I remember watching was in 2006, when it was held in Germany. At first, the sport was an escape from reality for me, an experience to share with other fellow fans, forget our issues, and fully immerse ourselves in a game for 90 minutes. Later I realized soccer is one of the few ways I can connect with the world. I grew up in China, and my need to consume soccer news and analysis is what drove me into journalism.

### Malaysia: Press Myanmar Junta Leader to End Abuses
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-06 23:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/06/malaysia-press-myanmar-junta-leader-to-end-abuses
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Myanmar nationals to be repatriated from Malaysia sit inside a bus, at Lumut, Malaysia, September 29, 2026. © 2026 Hasnoor Hussain/Reuters (Bangkok) – Malaysian Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim should press Myanmar’s junta leader, Min Aung Hlaing, to end human rights abuses during his visit to Kuala Lumpur on October 7, 2026, Human Rights Watch said today. The Malaysian government should also halt plans to forcibly return ethnic Rohingya and other people from Myanmar, where they face persecution and other rights violations. Malaysian authorities deported about 1,500 Myanmar nationals on September 29 in coordination with junta authorities, the first batch in a series of phased returns. “Malaysian Prime Minister Anwar Ibrahim’s invitation to Min Aung Hlaing to coordinate the forced return of people who fled military atrocities in Myanmar is an invitation for further abuse,” said Elaine Pearson, Asia director at Human Rights Watch.

### Mandate of the UN Special Rapporteur on Russia Extended for Another Year
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-06 18:31 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/06/mandate-of-the-un-special-rapporteur-on-russia-extended-for-another-year
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

The UN Human Rights Council on October 6 extended the mandate of the Special Rapporteur on the human rights situation in Russia for another year. Russia withdrew its amendment that attempted to delete renewal of the mandate. Click to expand Image The "Broken Chair” in front of the Palais Nations, Geneva, September 22, 2026. © 2026 Tanya Lokshina/Human Rights Watch The extension is welcome news. The Russia mandate provides an essential independent voice to document the Russian government’s grave human rights violations and serves as a lifeline for the country’s besieged civil society. The mandate was established in 2022 when, along with its war against Ukraine, the Kremlin launched a draconian crackdown on human rights defenders, independent journalists, political opponents, and other Russian based critics of the war. With the Ukraine war now in its fifth year, the Kremlin continues to escalate the crackdown on virtually all forms of dissent. There are over 1,800 political prisoners in Russia.

### Missing Lao Critic in Thailand Feared ‘Disappeared’
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-06 12:54 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/06/missing-lao-critic-in-thailand-feared-disappeared
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Khieo Lapchanh. © 2026 Khieo Lapchanh/Facebook Three weeks ago, 26-year-old Lao activist Khieo Lapchanh, an asylum seeker in Thailand, vanished in Samut Sakhon province following his shift at the restaurant where he worked. Khieo’s disappearance in the early hours of September 15 came one day before his first interview with the United Nations refugee agency (UNHCR) in Bangkok. Thai authorities have yet to provide any information regarding his whereabouts. Khieo had been posting videos critical of the Lao government to his Facebook page, Khiew Channel, which had about 158,000 followers. According to Manushya Foundation, a rights group that supported him, he had faced coordinated harassment, doxxing, and death threats. After Lao officials visited his mother's home in Laos this year on March 31 and April 17 concerning his activities, Khieo stopped posting political content. Given the threats he faced and the history of enforced disappearances of Lao activists both in Laos and abroad, Thai authorities should urgently locate him.

Click to expand Image A United Nations team visits with officials of Yemen's Supervisory Committee in Al Hudaydah province, Yemen, May 17, 2023. © 2023 Mohammed Hamoud/Getty Images (Beirut) – The Houthi de facto authorities in Yemen have sent at least eight people involved in human rights and humanitarian work for criminal prosecution, on espionage-related charges punishable by death, since August 18, 2026, Amnesty International and Human Rights Watch said today. They include two United Nations employees, a UN consultant, and five people who worked with international and local civil society organizations on a range of issues, including health, human rights, and humanitarian aid. The Houthis should immediately halt these prosecutions and unconditionally release the eight people whose cases were sent to the prosecution, as well as the scores of other UN staff and civil society workers the Houthis have arbitrarily detained over the past two years.

### Tunisia: Power, Water Shortages Endanger Rights
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-06 04:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/06/tunisia-power-water-shortages-endanger-rights
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Cars pass through a darkened street during a nighttime power outage in Ariana, Tunisia, July 14, 2026. © 2026 Chedly Ben Ibrahim/NurPhoto via AP Photo (Beirut) – Tunisian authorities failed to ensure an adequate supply of electricity and water during two months of extreme summer heat, undermining the right to health and endangering lives, Human Rights Watch said today. President Kais Saied blamed the shortages on sabotage, yet authorities failed to provide transparent information and instead cracked down on protesters. Rolling power outages left hospitals and water pumping stations without electricity and households without running water, interrupted critical medical procedures, and contributed to a nationwide shortage of drinking water. Doctors and human rights groups have reported a rise in heat-related hospitalizations and deaths exacerbated by the cuts, including among detainees.

### Sri Lanka: Counterterrorism Bill Retains Abusive Powers
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-06 01:30 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/05/sri-lanka-counterterrorism-bill-retains-abusive-powers
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image A police officer holds handcuffs as he walks inside a court premises in Colombo, Sri Lanka, August 6, 2026. © 2026 Thilina Kaluthotage/Reuters (Brussels) – The Sri Lankan government’s latest attempt to replace its abusive Prevention of Terrorism Act (PTA) retains numerous provisions that have enabled serious human rights violations, Human Rights Watch said today. The proposed Protection of the State from Terrorism Bill (PSTB), which will be sent to Parliament on October 6, 2026, includes an overbroad definition of terrorism, inadequate judicial oversight, sweeping powers of search and arrest for the armed forces, and executive authority to curtail freedom of speech, assembly, association, and movement. These provisions neither meet the benchmarks set out by United Nations human rights experts, nor comply with human rights obligations and commitments that Sri Lanka made to the European Union under the Generalized System of Preferences (GSP+), which links trade benefits to human rights conditions.

### High and Rising Institutional Concentration of Award-Winning Economists -- by Richard B. Freeman, Danxia Xie, Hanzhe Zhang, Hanzhang Zhou
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35875#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We analyze the academic affiliations of nearly 6,000 award-winning researchers in 18 major fields in the natural sciences, engineering, and social sciences from the 1820s to the 2020s, focusing on the 1960s onward. The analysis reveals a trend of declining concentration in the institutional affiliations of award-winning researchers, shifting from a few science-strong universities in high-income countries to a more diverse set of institutions across the world. The decline in concentration is observed in all fields except one: economics. The institutional affiliations of prizewinning economists have become more concentrated over time, making economics the most concentrated field. We associate the higher concentration of prizewinning work in economics with the field’s stronger sorting by institutional prestige, its lower reliance on specialized equipment and instruments, and its assessment of findings based on a synthesis of evidence rather than on decisive experiments or proofs.

### Tax Planning and Multinational Behavior -- by Rosanne Altshuler, Lysle Boller, Kevin A. Roberts, Juan Carlos Suárez Serrato
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35874#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We use newly linked IRS data to study how US multinationals use tax-planning structures that exploit mismatches between US and foreign tax laws. In 2016, these structures accounted for 36% of foreign profits despite being adopted by only 18% of firms. We develop and estimate a structural model using adoption patterns and changes in foreign effective tax rates, domestic and foreign investment, and global R&D following adoption. The model implies substantial heterogeneity in adoption costs and benefits and shows that restricting tax planning creates tradeoffs between tax revenue and economic activity that are amplified by global R&D linkages.

### In Utero Exposure to the 1982 Tylenol Recall and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes -- by Christopher M. Worsham, Charles Bray, Joshua Rothman, Brian Kwan, Anupam Jena
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35873#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Observational research into the relationship between maternal acetaminophen use during pregnancy and a child’s subsequent risk of attention deficit hyperactivity disorder (ADHD) or autism has been limited by confounding factors. Using two large national U.S. insurance claims databases and a novel natural experiment, we assessed differences in prevalence of ADHD and autism among children whose gestational periods were impacted by a nationwide Tylenol recall issued in 1982, following deaths from Tylenol capsules containing potassium cyanide. Prevalence of ADHD and autism was not significantly different in birth cohorts exposed to the recall during gestation, when maternal acetaminophen use would have been reduced; interrupted time series models showed no significant differences compared to underlying temporal diagnostic trends. These findings are inconsistent with a clinically meaningful effect of maternal acetaminophen use on subsequent development of neurodevelopmental disorders.

### Estimating Heterogeneous Impacts with Endogenous Participation and Non-Compliance -- by Orazio Attanasio, Lina Cardona-Sosa, Ziqian Liu
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35872#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This paper considers the estimation of heterogeneous impacts within a Randomized Controlled Trial (RCT) in a situation where heterogeneous status is endogenous and is not fully observed in the control sample. We show how to use information in the treatment sample and a noisy signal on the heterogeneous status to estimate meaningful impacts under a minimal set of assumptions. We provide an application of this approach to a data set from Ghana, collected around a clustered RCT to evaluate the impacts of an early-years intervention, differentiating the impact between households that participate actively in the program from those who participate only passively, as recipients. We show that, for some outcomes, the properly estimated heterogeneous impacts differ substantially from those computed with a naive estimator.

### The Effects of Long-Term Exposure to Pollution on Health -- by Joshua S. Graff Zivin, Johnny Huynh, Timothy Frank Justicz, Adriana Lleras-Muney, Matthew J. Neidell
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35871#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We provide the first causal evidence on how long-term exposure to fine particulate matter (PM2.5) at contemporary ambient levels contributes to the development of chronic disease in a young, initially healthy population. Our analysis links detailed personnel records for roughly 150,000 U.S. Army soldiers to high-resolution annual measures of PM2.5. Because soldiers enter service exceptionally healthy and receive uniform access to care, new diagnoses reflect pollution’s physiological impact rather than preexisting conditions or differential health care access. They are also required to relocate based on the needs of the Army, which provides us with quasi-random variation in initial duty-station assignments that we use as an instrument for average annual pollution exposure over a soldier’s career. We find that sustained PM2.5 exposure substantially increases circulatory disease, particularly hypertension, with mixed evidence on respiratory conditions that are sensitive to specification.

### Aligning Platform and User Incentives in Social Media Age Restrictions -- by Leonardo Bursztyn, Rafael Jiménez-Durán, Aaron Leonard, Filip Milojević, Christopher Roth
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35870#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Several countries are adopting social media bans for teenagers, yet early evidence suggests limited adherence to the age-based restrictions. Such bans ask platforms to remove profitable customers and teens to give up access to their online social networks. We study an alternative regulatory regime in which platforms remain liable for teens on the adult tier but may offer a safe tier that removes restricted features, such as algorithmic feeds, while preserving interactions with their relevant network. Provision of the safe tier and enforcement are complementary: teens can adhere without giving up access to their network, and platforms can retain displaced adult-tier users as lower-margin customers. This policy design makes the teen decision to avoid the adult tier less dependent on peer dynamics and increases teen welfare for a relevant range of regulatory penalties.

### Not all geopolitical shocks are inflationary
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-06 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/not-all-geopolitical-shocks-are-inflationary
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Geopolitical tensions are often seen as a source of inflation. But this is only part of the story. This column shows that geopolitical shocks transmitted through energy markets raise prices while depressing economic activity. But geopolitical shocks unrelated to energy markets reduce inflation instead. These two shocks can be identified based on oil prices and geopolitical risk dynamics around major geopolitical events.

### Worker-managed firms can curb inequality and protect free markets
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-06 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/worker-managed-firms-can-curb-inequality-and-protect-free-markets
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Rising income inequality in Western high-income countries threatens democratic governance and social cohesion, and AI risks making it worse. Drawing on a forthcoming book, this column argues for worker-managed firms as a durable remedy. With no external owners taking profits, worker cooperatives protect against the rising bargaining power of employers and provide an enhanced sense of self-worth and self-determination for employees. Furthermore, the data suggest that well-designed worker cooperatives compete with conventional firms in free markets with no evidence of a productivity gap. Because they are underprovided by the market, it calls for government action to support worker cooperatives.

### Venture capital – a global perspective
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-05 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/venture-capital-global-perspective
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Existing views on what venture capital finances and how its markets are structured are primarily shaped by the experience of the US and a few other high-income economies. This column presents evidence covering nearly 270,000 venture capital-backed startups in over 150 economies to provide a more complete picture. Venture capital markets differ across countries in size and also in kind. At lower levels of economic development, the composition tilts away from knowledge intangibles towards organisational intangibles. Yet differences in what venture capital finances tell only part of the story. Most of the cross-country gap in venture capital activity is associated with how many startups enter the funding pipeline rather than how much each raises.

### Apprendre aux débutants à réfléchir à l’ère des grands modèles linguistiques
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-05 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/apprendre-aux-debutants-reflechir-lere-des-grands-modeles-linguistiques
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

La question de savoir si l’enseignement des compétences cognitives reste pertinent à l’ère de l’IA est cruciale tant pour le monde de l’éducation que pour les entreprises. Cet article rend compte d’une expérience menée auprès d’environ 1 000 étudiants de premier cycle en Italie, qui ont reçu soit (1) une formation au raisonnement causal, (2) un accès à ChatGPT, (3) les deux, soit (4) aucun des deux. Si les étudiants ayant utilisé ChatGPT ont obtenu de meilleurs résultats lors d’un devoir consistant à rédiger une recommandation de 180 mots sur un problème concret, seuls ceux ayant suivi la formation au raisonnement causal ont proposé des idées originales. Ces résultats soulignent les avantages de combiner une formation à la réflexion par principes fondamentaux avec l’utilisation de grands modèles linguistiques.

### Bonding through crises with nonbank financial institutions
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-04 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/bonding-through-crises-nonbank-financial-institutions
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Nonbank financial institutions are often seen as procyclical lenders, cutting credit more sharply than banks during stress, while bond markets can substitute for bank lending when credit supply contracts. This column brings these perspectives together and argues that this cyclicality depends on the function intermediaries perform. During banking crises, nonbank financial institutions contract syndicated lending relative to banks but expand corporate bond underwriting. For firms with potential access to bond markets, we find that relationships with nonbank underwriters can therefore help preserve market-based financing when bank credit is impaired.

### The impact of AI and cross-border data regulation on international trade in digital services
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-03 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/impact-ai-and-cross-border-data-regulation-international-trade-digital-services
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

AI is transforming digital services, and mobile apps, which routinely reach users far beyond their home markets, show just how global that transformation has become. This column shows that AI makes these products dramatically more attractive, increasing their user base at home and abroad by more than threefold. But when governments restrict the movement of data across borders, the gains from AI are halved. This creates a genuine policy tension. Rules designed to protect privacy, national security, and democratic institutions can also make it harder to build better products and spread the gains from new technologies.

Normal AI posting should resume tomorrow, likely with a report from the conference. Is Our Children Learning The declines in NAEP scores and other tests seem to be concentrated on the weakest students. The bottom of the distribution is falling through the floor and the rest is mostly holding up. This does not match anecdotal reports coming out of schools, which suggest widespread declines, although those are presumably untrustworthy and it’s traditional to think children are in general getting dumber. ### AI Safety at the Frontier: Paper Highlights of August & September 2026
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-06 20:20 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/sxtB288gm2tAaLfNu/ai-safety-at-the-frontier-paper-highlights-of-august-and
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

tl;dr Paper of the month: Plain reinforcement learning (RL) on real, hackable training tasks produces a reward-seeking model that takes harmful actions to raise its reward, while its headline score in standard safety audits barely moves. Research highlights: A probe on a model’s internal activations detects reward hacking in long coding transcripts roughly as well as a generically prompted LLM monitor. Debate with a weak LLM judge and a critic (trained in parallel) keeps the judge much more accurate and reduces reward hacking in RL. Automated researchers based on Claude Opus 4.8 successfully invent training methods to improve on ten alignment misbehaviors, beating researchers’ one-shot ideas. Researchers successfully use evolutionary search to find mind viruses, prompts that persuade LLM agents to pass them on. However, they are still brittle, and a short warning in the system prompt effectively stops them.

But primarily this post serves as a mildly curated reading list of texts by others making similar points. I’ll also give some examples of conceptual/fuzzy/… work in alignment research. In my mind, an important fact about alignment research (e.g., for figuring out how to automate it, for how to design work tests, how to organize the research community) is that it has in part the following cluster of properties: preparadigmatic; philosophical; conceptual; fuzzy; doesn’t have clear metrics/success criteria (yet); doesn’t have well-defined open problems; requires lots of speculation that is never tested before it’s too late; often ultimately bottoms out in human intuition and judgement. In the following I’ll use “conceptual” as a stand-in for the whole cluster. Note that some of these properties seem pretty different from each other. ### If cleantech funding is scarce, does founding another startup actually help the world?
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-06 16:45 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/Z7TcwNzzndxn7tBc9/if-cleantech-funding-is-scarce-does-founding-another-startup
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

For context, I’m a student trying to choose a career where they can have maximum impact. One path I’m considering is founding a cleantech startup. However, I’m worried that even if I found a startup, I might not actually increase the total amount of socially impactful work being done in the world. Here’s my reasoning: suppose, very crudely, that the total social benefit produced by the cleantech sector scales with the number of cleantech startups that get funded. Also suppose there are more "viable" startups (i.e. startups that could plausibly become profitable if adequately financed) seeking funding than there are funding opportunities. If so, funding my company may simply displace capital from another viable startup. I would change which company gets funded, but not how many get funded, and therefore have little to no counterfactual impact. I’ve considered a few possible counterarguments: i) My startup could be more socially valuable than the one it displaces.

### Reading habits for the new literary ‘dark ages’
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-06 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/the-new-literary-dark-ages/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

The 18th-century writer and polymath Samuel Johnson was seldom seen without a book. People say he read in bed and while walking. He even read at dinner, the pages folded over his lap like a “dog who holds a bone in his paws in reserve while he eats something else,” as his biographer, James Boswell, put it . The fact that today, Johnson is perhaps best known as the face of an internet meme — which, ironically, shows him looking at a text in annoyed befuddlement — tells us pretty much everything we need to know about how reading habits have changed since. As The Times columnist James Marriott suggests in the subtitle of his latest book, The New Dark Ages: The Death of Reading and the Dawn of the Post-Literate Society , what was once part of most people’s daily routines is slowly becoming a lost art, supplanted first by the audiovisual allure of television and now short-form content on social media. Marriott is far from the first to write about this topic.

### Pure sulfur discovered on Mars, making Martian concrete possible
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-06 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/sulfur-mars-martian-concrete-possible/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

If we ever hope to build a base, a city, or a full-fledged colony on another world, the first steps are to build structures that can support human existence. The Moon is the closest option, but it has many limitations: its gravity is greatly reduced, it has practically no atmosphere at all, and nearly all of the resources humans would need must be brought from Earth. Mars is a little more interesting, with substantial reserves of (solid and gaseous) water, a different mineralogy from Earth, and a thin but meaningful atmosphere: the eighth thickest in the Solar System , just one spot behind Earth. Importantly, Mars is a sand-rich world, and with sand, an interesting possibility arises as far as construction materials go: concrete, with sand serving as an aggregate . In the past, studies have shown that the type of sand found on Mars is made of a very different composition than sand on Earth, and with a different grain size as well.

### The leading theory of how life began just hit a snag
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-05 14:07 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/science-tech/the-leading-theory-of-how-life-began-just-hit-a-snag/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

How and where life first emerged on Earth is one of the great unsolved puzzles for scientists exploring the possibility of life on other worlds. For the past decade or so, the prevailing theory has been that the first primitive organisms originated at alkaline hydrothermal vents in the deep ocean. Now, a new paper by Benjamin Tutolo from the University of Calgary in Canada, published in the Proceedings of the National Academy of Sciences , challenges that assumption. There’s solid reasoning behind the old paradigm. High temperatures at hydrothermal vents, like the famed Lost City Hydrothermal Field on the Atlantic Ocean floor , are critical for organic molecules to come together to form the all-important biomolecules without which life cannot exist. Sulfide, the reduced form of sulfur used to build cell membranes, was also thought to be plentiful in these deep-ocean environments. It seemed the perfect place for nonliving materials to transform into the first living cell.

Planet Earth possesses many impressively large mountains. This map shows the number of bodies located near the summit of Mt. Everest, the highest point above sea level on the surface of planet Earth. Although it was long thought that terrain would be the most significant contributing factor to deaths on the mountain, the distribution of bodies shows that the main culprit is not terrain, but rather elevation that is the determining factor. Credit : pointofnoreturn.org Mount Everest represents the surface’s highest point, peaking 8.849 km (29,032 feet) above sea level. Even as viewed from space, captured here from the International Space Station, Mauna Kea’s summit is an incredibly impressive site. From sea level, it rises up 4,205 meters (13,800 feet) above the oceans, but sinks an additional 5000 to 6000 meters down to the sea floor, making it the highest mountain, base-to-peak, of any feature on Earth. It is thought to be completely extinct, having last erupted more than 4000 years ago.

### Everyone in the room thinks they’re the only one struggling
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-03 15:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/work-wise/everyone-in-the-room-thinks-theyre-the-only-one-struggling/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

The connective ritual behind my closest friendships and the best teams asks the same question: What are you working on that you need help with? During my PhD, I regularly participated in something called Shut Up and Write. The idea was (wait for it) to be quiet and work, but in a room full of people doing the same. Two hours, sometimes three, broken into 25-minute blocks with five-minute breaks and coffee in between. I went to my first one because I was stuck, but also because I was promised free coffee. New to the Australian version of go-fast bean juice, I happily accepted the invitation but was skeptical that I needed a room full of strangers to help me work. So I showed up to this little café buried on the top floor of the music school, eight people huddled around a table with screens up and cords everywhere. And we got down to work. Something interesting happened during the first break. People around the table asked, “What are you working on?” The woman across from me was three months into a chapter rewrite she hated. Somebody else had been stuck in the lab for most of the year.

### 3 social skills you should never outsource to AI
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-02 15:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/mind-behavior/social-skills-you-should-never-outsource-to-ai/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

“AI is helping me with my social skills,” one of my therapy clients told me. He experienced social anxiety and had started asking AI for scripts to say whenever he felt nervous. At a recent dinner party, when he felt overwhelmed by all the insightful questions the other guests were asking, he slipped away for a restroom break. He asked AI to create a list of questions that would make him a more interesting conversationalist so he could be well-liked. He returned to the dinner table armed with questions and a little more confidence. Like many people, he hoped AI would hand him a social shortcut, one that would make him less anxious in the long run. Unfortunately, his AI use was actually hampering his social growth. While those scripts gave him a momentary confidence boost, they were making him less socially adept over time. As a therapist, I’m seeing my people hand over three common social practices to AI, and it’s simultaneously eroding their confidence and social skills.

### Bumper-to-Bumper Traffic
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-05 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/bumper-to-bumper-traffic
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

2026 Toronto International Film Festival sponsorship ad by Rogers. At Cannes, competition screenings are preceded by nothing more than a computer-generated set of red-carpet stairs ascending out of the sea, sans any credits for financial supporters or slogans, in keeping with the festival’s self-image as cinema itself, purged of anything inessential. (This conception isn’t true, but it’s a nice, self-burnishing illusion about the world’s most prestigious festival.) This is a flex: To not have to mention sponsors is a self-presentation of financial invincibility. Less affluent concerns must acknowledge sponsors in their opening bumpers (in-house commercials branding both the festival and its supporters), albeit briskly , as in Sundance’s graphics about storytelling shifting to very quick cards listing institutional and individual sponsors. At the bottom of the scale is Tribeca Festival, the for-profit entity which treats audiences as multiplex captives, subjected to a lengthy pre-screening reel of commercials before an introduction, followed by even more ads prior to the film.

### Life at Its Wildest: Werner Herzog on “Bucking Fastard”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-01 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/life-at-its-wildest-werner-herzog-on-bucking-fastard
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

From left to right: Cinematographer Peter Zeitlinger, director Werner Herzog, and stars Rooney Mara and Kate Mara shooting Bucking Fastard (2026). Courtesy of Gateway to Orkney LLC. Werner Herzog’s first fiction film in seven years, Bucking Fastard (2026),stands as one of his most bizarre. That’s high praise for a director who’s pulled 300-plus-ton steamships over mountains ( Fitzcarraldo , 1982), hypnotized his actors ( Heart of Glass , 1976), and followed “deranged” penguins across Antarctica ( Encounters at the End of the World , 2007). Herzog’s entire oeuvre teems with drifters pursuing quixotic dreams, people and stories often so odd as to strain credulity. But rarely has that strangeness felt more entrancing than it does here. Like his previous foray into fiction, 2019’s Family Romance, LLC —inspired by a real-life Japanese company renting actors to stand in for any person its clients desire— Bucking Fastard dramatizes a true story into a kind of fairytale.

### Members Only: Cabal-Core Horror in the 21st Century
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-09-29 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/members-only-cabal-core-horror-in-the-21st-century
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Salò: Or the 120 Days of Sodom (Pier Paolo Pasolini, 1975). In 2005, during a television interview in Italy, Giuseppe “Pino” Pelosi—AKA “The Frog”—recanted his confession about murdering Pier Paolo Pasolini on a beach in Ostia 30 years earlier. He claimed that it had been extracted by coercion and threats of violence, against both himself and his family (Pelosi was seventeen at the time). He also said that the legendary director’s death was a political killing disguised as a crime of passion—the premeditated and methodical muzzling of a “Dirty Communist.” Sergio Citti, a close friend of Pasolini, contributed to the controversy by suggesting that the filmmaker had been lured to the water under the guise of retrieving stolen reels of film from his transgressive—and, as it turned out, valedictory—magnum opus Salò: Or the 120 Days of Sodom (1975)—a savage critique of his homeland’s then-recent legacy of fascist decadence. “All’s good if it’s excessive,” says a religious figurehead early in Salò, describing both his unchecked fetishism and the M.O. of the film around him.

Home video release images from The Good, the Bad and the Ugly (Sergio Leone, 1966). Clockwise from top-left: Arrow Video (4K UHD, 2025), Kino Lorber (4K UHD, 2021), MGM (4K Blu-ray, 2014), MGM (DVD, 1998). “ People who alter or destroy works of art and our cultural heritage for profit or as an exercise of power are barbarians, and if the laws of the United States continue to condone this behavior, history will surely classify us as a barbaric society. ” —George Lucas, 1988 “ To me, it [the original Star Wars trilogy] doesn’t really exist anymore. It’s like this is the movie I wanted it to be, and I’m sorry you saw half a completed film and fell in love with it.” —George Lucas, 2004 Vancouver, 2011 : My then-roommate Willa Ross and I are at our wit’s end in one of Simon Fraser University’s characteristically antiseptic editing suites, having just spent the weekend hacking together three distinct copies of Sergio Leone’s 1966 western The Good, the Bad and the Ugly , all illegally ripped.

### Movie Poster of the Week | The Posters of the 1976 New York Film Festival
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-09-25 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/movie-poster-of-the-week-the-posters-of-the-1976-new-york-film-festival
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

French grande for Small Change (François Truffaut, France, 1976). Design by Jouineau Bourduge. Another year, another Truffaut, another Fassbinder, another Visconti, another Renoir revival. The 1976 New York Film Festival—which ran from October 1 to 17—was nothing if not true to its brand. But the 14th New York Film Festival, programmed by Richard Roud, Susan Sontag, Richard Corliss, Charles Michener, and Roger Greenspun, was still a relatively small affair, showcasing just eighteen features and eleven shorts. It was also still a defiantly Euro-centric program, top heavy with French cinema. Both the opening- and closing-night films were French (François Truffaut’s Small Change and Eric Rohmer’s The Marquise of O , respectively) and there were four other French features in the mix along with an Agnès Varda short. There were three films from Germany, two from Italy, and one from Switzerland.

Get in touch to send in cinephile news and discoveries. To keep up with our latest features, sign up for the Weekly Edit newsletter and follow us @mubinotebook on X and Instagram . NEWS Woman Unknown (May el-Toukhy, 2026). The Maggie Gyllenhaal-led jury at the 83rd Venice International Film Festival announced its list of winners . May el-Toukhy’s Woman Unknown (2026) won the Golden Lion and Lee Chang-dong’s Possible Love (2026) won the Grand Jury Prize. Meanwhile, Javier Calvo and Javier Ambrossi’s La Bola Negra (2026) won the People’s Choice Award at this year’s Toronto International Film Festival. Paramount CEO David Ellison has resolved the antitrust lawsuit filed by twelve states opposing the acquisition of Warner Bros. Discovery, paving the way for the merger to go forward. The news has prompted mixed reactions from the industry : while the DGA, SAG-AFTRA, and IATSE broadly support the settlement, the WGA still believes the merger to be a mistake.

The record has been newly remastered for its updated release, and will be made available on vinyl and CD. A digital release of the remaster is currently not scheduled to happen. Originally released in June 1993, Boces was Mercury Rev's second studio album, and was their final LP to feature original frontman David Baker, who was asked to leave the band shortly after they finished touring the record. Jeremy Allen explores the bombed-out terrain of their West Berlin and wonders if, despite the penurious conditions, it was easier to make art then Feral musicians emerging from the rubble of bombed-out West Berlin (as composer György Ligeti described it, "a surrealist cage in which all those inside are free") and creating themselves out of their own blood and guts very much feels like a tale from another age. In a world of coffee shop startups, utilitarian computer software and ersatz bedroom electronica as aural wallpaper, building your own percussive instruments from scrap might seem primitive, complex, redundant even. ### Premiere: Ava Rasti’s The River
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-05 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/video/premiere-ava-rasti-s-the-river
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Watch an exclusive premiere of the film of Ava Rasti’s The River, first performed as part of the 2025 Semibreve festival. The River by Iranian-born Berlin-based composer Ava Rasti was commissioned by Portuguese festival Semibreve and first presented on 25 October 2025 at the Capela Imaculada do Seminário Menor in Braga. The performance brought together Rasti and a string quartet composed of Filipa Abreu (violin), Vânia Oliveira (viola), Tiago Mendes (cello) and Catarina Coelho (cello). The work takes the Piave river in northern Italy as its inspiration, a landscape in which the present is intertwined with the traces of wars and violence from its past. Rasti constructs these fragmented memories, recontextualised and transformed into sounds. Wire subscribers can read Claire Sawer's review of Semibreve 2025 here .

### “A trip through the other side of enlightenment”: Funkadelic reviewed
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-01 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/essays/a-trip-through-the-other-side-of-enlightenment-funkadelic-reviewed
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Two newly reissued albums showcase the seismic psychedelia of George Clinton’s Funkadelic at its rawest and most disruptive, writes John Morrison in The Wire 512 Funkadelic Free Your Mind And Your Ass Will Follow Westbound/Org CD/DL/LP/2xLP/MC Maggot Brain Westbound/Org CD/DL/LP/2xLP/MC Released the summer before Jimi Hendrix tragically passed away in 1970, Free Your Mind And Your Ass Will Follow was not only the follow-up to Funkadelic’s promising self-titled debut of the same year; it signalled the arrival of George Clinton’s ensemble as the next great voice in Black rock and psychedelia. In his 2014 memoir Brothas Be, Yo Like George, Ain’t That Funkin’ Kinda Hard On You? , Clinton acknowledges this by contrasting the album’s release with Hendrix’s death, an event that he notes as “the end of an era”. During his brief but electrifying career, Hendrix moulded multiple aspects of the Black musical tradition – blues, rock, funk, R&B, jazz – into an electrifying corpus that opened up a new world of possibilities.

### Wire mix: Kaja Draksler
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-09-24 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/audio/tracks/wire-mix-kaja-draksler
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

To complement her interview in The Wire 512, Slovenian improvising pianist and bandleader Kaja Draksler creates an exclusive Wire mix Kaja Draksler studied jazz at a music-oriented high school in Ljubljana, later enrolling at the Conservatory of Amsterdam. There, her instructor, the British composer Richard Ayres, asked her the simple question,“What do you want to do?”, which set her on a path that would chafe against the jazz orthodoxy. In August Punkt.Vrt. Plastik, her long-running trio with Swedish bassist Petter Eldh and German drummer Christian Lillinger, released their fourth album, Kompress , while another of her groups, matter 100, releases its debut True Or False this October. This exclusive mix, she explains, is “more of a collage, where I mostly take excerpts from different pieces, placing them one after the other, and, at times, one of top of the other. The track selection is a mix of my own music and music I love, with a bit of a focus on piano and voices.

### Miramar Tower / OODA
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-06 13:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1034516/miramar-tower-ooda
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Standing out for its uniqueness in the landscape and for its proximity to the sea, Miramar Tower is similar to the buildings in Pasteleira Park to the north, in proportion and dimension, but otherwise completely distinctive in its concept approach. The volumetric composition is the result of the architectural drive with the ambition to develop housing typologies with more than 200 square meters of balcony areas per floor. On top of the podium, a vertical volume with 15 storeys emerges, parallel to the road and with roughly the same height as the tower buildings in this area. By locating the building in the most central part of the plot, it ensures maximum spacing for all adjacent buildings. Evidencing a structuralist matrix with vertical dendriform section, the building is adaptable to the typology optimization. The floors, organized with one or two rooms, create a spiral speech where the apparent rotation and modelling of the terraces at 360 degrees give it a visual and real sense of verticality.

Boo to that in the real world, obviously, but it's a narrative device that can make for some interesting storytelling, and according to the game's narrative designer, that storytelling manifests in being able to wrap up investigations with hardly any evidence. ### Effects of land acknowledgements on non-Indigenous audiences in Australia and the USA
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02520-6
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 25 September 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02520-6 McNamee and Peyton find that land acknowledgements raise awareness of, but not curiosity about, Indigenous nations. These practices improve organizational reputations, but their effects on political attitudes and support for reparations are insignificant.

### AI amplifies experts, not expertise
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02604-3
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 29 September 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02604-3 Acquiring knowledge and expertise is a long process. Today, artificial intelligence (AI) tools can amplify what people are able to do, but this may pose problems for the development of the next generation of experts, argues Sheng-Tun Li.

### Introducing AI to an online petition platform changed outputs but not outcomes
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02580-8
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 30 September 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02580-8 Corpus et al. examine how the introduction of an AI writing tool impacted Change.org petitions, finding that it increased petition homogeneity but did not improve petition outcomes.

### Intrinsic rewards guide visual resource allocation via reinforcement learning
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02573-7
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 01 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02573-7 Tangible rewards are known to sharpen visual processing of associated objects. Tomić et al. show that an internal sense of confidence—acting as an intrinsic reward—similarly biases attention, revealing how the brain prioritizes limited resources.

### Selfish and prosocial individuals both distort their actions’ impact, but selfish distortions can be more contagious
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02585-3
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 01 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02585-3 In this study, Vollberg and Gross show that both selfish and prosocial individuals exaggerate the social benefits of their choices, with selfish-serving distortions proving to be socially contagious.

### Gendered motives for group formidability among children and adults
Source: Nature Human Behaviour
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41562-026-02606-1
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Nature Human Behaviour, Published online: 02 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41562-026-02606-1 Fonn et al. find that boys but not girls prefer larger groups by age three, and these preferences predict support for group-based hierarchy among adults. This suggests that gendered motives for intergroup formidability are rooted in earliest childhood.

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Research by INRAE and AgroParisTech suggests that global warming could have an overall positive effect on sorghum yields in Europe. Sorghum is a promising alternative to maize in livestock feed. Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Physicists at CERN’s Large Hadron Collider have captured the clearest evidence yet that quark-gluon plasma, the scorching-hot matter that filled the newborn universe, behaves like a true liquid. By tracking individual quarks as they blasted through the plasma, researchers saw them leave behind ripples, splashes, and swirling wakes much like a duck moving through water. The finding suggests this primordial “soup” is so dense that it can slow speeding quarks and respond collectively as a fluid.

## academic research / 学术研究

### Occupational hazard incident reporting systems in health facilities across sub-Saharan Africa: A scoping review protocol
Source: PLOS ONE Biology
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0360189
Source meta: journal · primary · R4 · P4 · signal

by Innocent Ayesiga, David Tenywa, Moses Ntaro Introduction Occupational hazards (OH) in healthcare facilities are common, with 1 in 10 healthcare workers experiencing a hazard each year. These hazards can be either biological, chemical, ergonomic, or radiation. Despite their occurrence, reporting of these hazards remains limited, with very little information available, especially from healthcare facilities. Also, there is limited information on incident reporting systems (IRSs), which can help capture, track, and monitor the occurrence of these hazards within health facilities. The absence of these systems results in inaccurate and incomplete reporting and fragmented data, hindering the implementation of effective occupational safety measures in hospitals. This review will explore the extent and nature of information available on occupational hazard incident reporting systems in Sub-Saharan Africa.

### Eastern origin and three-millennia persistence of a founding grapevine lineage in Iberian viticulture
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01090-0?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Pérez-Jordà et al. use archaeogenomics to show that eastern domesticated grapevines laid the foundations for Iberian viticulture 3,000 years ago. Genetic relatedness indicates these introductions gave rise to extant Iberian cultivars through a few sexual generations, revealing clonal propagation of the cultivar Heben from the 10th century to the present.

### Ketamine attenuates habenula activity in response to aversive outcomes during Pavlovian learning
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01095-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Translating a preclinical model to humans using 7-tesla MRI, Pulcu et al. show that ketamine reduces habenula activity for the expectation of aversive outcomes during Pavlovian learning. This provides direct translational evidence for how ketamine modulates the neural circuitry implicated in depression, relevant to its rapid antidepressant effects.

### Cambrian fossils illuminate the ambulacrarian common ancestor
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01104-8?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

He et al. describe Yujingia, a new early Cambrian ambulacrarian with differentiated appendages that include pinnate arms with filter-feeding grooves. The morphology and phylogenetic position of Yujingia suggest that modern ambulacrarians, including echinoderms and hemichordates, evolved from a tentaculate, semi-sessile suspension-feeding ancestor.

### Quantifying the control laws governing terminal attack in lions
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01196-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Duporge and Frost combine drone videography with AI-based pose estimation to reconstruct the kinematics of 67 lion (Panthera leo) attacks. Speed is adjusted to regulate lateral acceleration during turns, while lion steering is guided by proportional navigation and proportional pursuit, a mixed guidance law previously identified only in aerial predators.

### Thirst reweights temperature preference through dual dopaminergic and endocrine modulation in Drosophila
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01114-0?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Chiang et al. show that thirst shifts Drosophila toward cooler temperatures by bidirectionally reweighting cold and hot responses in the mushroom body. Dopamine- and 20E-mediated pathways differentially tune these responses, which converge on a common avoidance-promoting output.

### Spatial proteomics reveals carboxysome adaptation and mitochondrial proteome in Paulinella chromatophora
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01113-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Using LOPIT-DC-based spatial proteomics, Cadena et al. map the sub-organellar protein landscape of Paulinella chromatophora, a cercozoan with evolutionary-early-stage photosynthetic organelles. They find nucleus-encoded carboxysome-associated proteins potentially forming a novel O2-scavenging system that may enhance carbon fixation efficiency. The mitochondrial sub-proteome reveals conserved membrane organization alongside divergent, lineage-specific proteins.

### An Oryza orphan gene confers trans-species drought tolerance
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00803-2?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

ROAD1 is an Oryza-specific orphan gene that co-opts conserved ABA signaling to enhance drought tolerance without obvious growth defects or yield penalty under normal conditions. Elite rice lines carrying its functional allele exhibited higher grain yield than controls under drought, and its heterologous expression enhanced drought tolerance across diverse angiosperms.

### Co-option of retrotransposons promotes antibody diversification
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00819-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

A longstanding mystery in antibody diversification is how AID specifically targets immunoglobulin V genes. Ancient LINE-1 retrotransposons embedded within the immunoglobulin locus provide a recruitment platform for the HUSH complex and AID to drive somatic hypermutation.

### Metabolic atlas of early human cortex reveals glycolytic remodeling and pentose phosphate pathway control of cell fate transitions
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00821-4?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

The developing human cortex is composed of diverse cell types, but how nutrient metabolism shapes their fate has remained unclear. This study maps the metabolic landscape of the developing human cortex and identifies glycolysis and pentose phosphate pathway activity as key regulators of radial glia fate, controlling the generation of outer radial glia and inhibitory neurons.

### Functional atlas of vertebrate viral RNA elements that stabilize RNA and enhance translation
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00870-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Functional profiling of ∼200,000 genomic segments from vertebrate-infecting viruses uncovers RNA elements that boost gene expression by stabilizing mRNAs and enhancing translation, revealing the diversity of TENT4-dependent mixed tailing and identifying canonical poly(A) polymerase-mediated cytoplasmic polyadenylation, together expanding our understanding of post-transcriptional regulation and RNA therapeutic design.

### [Series] Peripheral arterial disease: adverse impacts, disparities in outcomes, and the path ahead
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00847-0/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

This is the third paper in the peripheral arterial disease (PAD) Series. The first and second papers in this Series outline that PAD affects over 300 million people worldwide and can be diagnosed by non-invasive tests, as well as that treatment requires medications to reduce modifiable risk factors and that exercise therapy and revascularisation are required in selected patients. In this paper, the devastating effects of PAD at the individual level are described, including chronic pain, functional limitation, loss of independence, impaired physical and mental quality of life, unemployment, and financial instability.

### [Series] Current advancements in the care of peripheral arterial disease: modern approaches to medical, endovascular, and surgical treatment
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00845-7/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

People with peripheral arterial disease (PAD) of the lower limbs are at very high risk of cardiovascular events and therefore the cornerstone of their management is medical therapy and behaviour modification to reduce that risk. The risk of limb loss (through major amputation) is low in people with asymptomatic PAD or intermittent claudication with only a few patients progressing to the most severe form of PAD: chronic limb threatening ischaemia. Supervised exercise therapy is effective in patients with intermittent claudication to improve their walking capacity and avoids the risks associated with revascularisation procedures to improve blood flow to the limb.

### [Series] Understanding peripheral arterial disease in the current era
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-06 23:34 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)00846-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease is the primary driver of premature death in global populations in recent decades. Mortality statistics, most publications, and guidelines in the atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease field have traditionally focused on cardiac and cerebrovascular adverse events and causes of death. However, given the increasing prevalence of lower extremity peripheral arterial disease and its expanding burden on global health systems, such an emphasis might represent a skewed perception of vascular risk within the broader atherosclerotic spectrum.

### Efficient Analog-Initialized Latent Transport for Spatially Coherent Probabilistic Downscaling from Global to Kilometer Scales
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-06 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03736
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.03736v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Probabilistic downscaling must represent kilometer-scale structure left unresolved by a deterministic regional prediction. We introduce an analog-initialized latent transport method that uses historical regional residuals as a meteorologically informed empirical source. A frozen graph neural network predicts the deterministic state from the Integrated Forecasting System (IFS). For each input, residuals from 20 training dates with similar IFS conditions initialize the ensemble. A conditional flow-matching model transports these fields in a carefully designed 65-factor, 100,000-node latent space, and a grid decoder reconstructs 81 atmospheric channels including precipitation at 2.5km spacing. On a season- and cycle-balanced 2025 test, ensemble CRPS improves over deterministic mean absolute error for all 81 variables. Matched analogs improve point and spatial errors over Gaussian initialization and reduce spectral error compared with covariance matching.

### Short-term solar eclipse predictions using Euclidean geometry and dynamical systems
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-06 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03737
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.03737v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We propose a computationally inexpensive algorithm to determine whether an opaque spherical body eclipses a luminous one, using only classical geometrical tools. We apply this method to the Sun-Earth-Moon system and predict solar eclipses, including their type, beginning, and duration, using the three-body problem. For predictions over a one-year period, the resulting errors are in the order of minutes.

### Deep Learning Denoising of Real SWOT Sea Surface Height Observations
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-06 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03739
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.03739v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: The SWOT (Surface Water Ocean Topography) mission is currently providing unpreceded high-resolution measurements of Sea Surface Height (SSH), revealing ocean features at finer scales. Nevertheless, the two-dimensional observations of KaRIn altimeter of SWOT suffer from instrumental errors. This noise degradation is altering the high frequencies of SWOT signal, and the small to sub-mesoscale dynamics of interest for some oceanographers. For this reason, Tr\'eboutte et al. (2023) have developed a convolutional neural network (CNN) based on U-Net architecture to separate the noise from the physical signals contained in the SSH. Their approach has demonstrated great potential on simulated SWOT measurements, and Dibarboure et al. (2024) report a positive influence on actual flight data from SWOT. However, degraded denoising performance, and occasional negative side-effects have been observed in atypical conditions (e.g. very high surface waves, internal tides solitons).

### MLS and CALIPSO Evidence for a Warm-Pool Transition in Tropical Convective Moistening and Cloud Ice
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-06 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03742
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.03742v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Upper-tropospheric water vapor and cloud ice associated with tropical deep convection are central to water-vapor and high-cloud feedbacks. We investigate a warm-pool transition in tropical convective moistening using 20 years of Aura Microwave Limb Sounder (MLS) Version 5 H2O and cloud-ice observations (August 2004-December 2024), together with Cloud-Aerosol Lidar with Orthogonal Polarization (CALIOP) aboard the Cloud-Aerosol Lidar and Infrared Pathfinder Satellite Observations (CALIPSO) mission (June 2006-June 2023). The combined day-plus-night CALIPSO product (CALIPSO-A) provides an independent ice-cloud constraint; the H2O column shown on the CALIPSO grid is derived from Modern-Era Retrospective Analysis for Research and Applications, Version 2 (MERRA-2), not from CALIOP.

### Structured Neural Modeling of Daily Arctic Sea-Ice Concentration Evolution: Physical-Trajectory-Driven Learning and Forecast-Domain Adaptation
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-06 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03743
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.03743v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Accurate modeling of the daily evolution of sea ice concentration (SIC) is central to improving the credibility and operational forecasting capability of deep learning-based sea ice prediction. However, existing deep learning methods often couple the underlying sea ice evolution relationships and data errors within high-dimensional nonlinear mappings, making it difficult to construct a stable and verifiable evolution core and apply it reliably to practical forecasting. To address this issue, this study proposes a reanalysis-forecast dual-domain decoupled framework for learning sea ice evolution operators. The framework builds upon a lightweight physical baseline to generate daily evolution trajectories, employs a temporally constrained joint multi-lead compensation network to com?pensate for unresolved processes, and introduces an ice-mass?aware transport mechanism to suppress numerical dissipation.

### Black-Scholes for the land: modelling savannah response to rain as a dynamic world model
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-06 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03744
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.03744v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Carbon credits, offsets and index insurance rest on counterfactuals: what the land would have done under the same weather without management, or what a bad season cost. Current baselines fail in two ways: some ignore rain, others condition on rain linearly and annually. But the true response lags the rain, depends on hidden state such as moisture from other sources, and responds differently from pixel to pixel. Focussing on grassland pasture as a proof of concept, we fit a temporal world model of MODIS greenness conditioned on CHIRPS rain over two 45 km^2 sites in Laikipia, Kenya: a transition density map for greenness given recent and current rainfall. We use it, conceptually, as Black-Scholes is used. Inverting the model on observed greenness it yields an implied residual, everything the land did that the rain does not explain. Two capabilities drop out of this solution: 1) attribution, what the land did beyond the rain; 2) sensitivities, derivatives of the rollout with respect to rain and state, close analogues of the Greeks.

### To Keep GPS Constellations From Drifting, Look To The Moon
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-06 11:09 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/to-keep-gps-constellations-from-drifting-look-to-the-moon
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Global Positioning Systems are a key feature in everyday life for much of the world’s population. Whether it’s ensuring a ship delivers its goods safely, or you’re able to avoid a road closure on your commute home, GPS can help. But the system has a weakness - it relies on ground stations that could suffer from blackouts, natural disasters, or cyber attacks. And if those ground stations go offline for long enough, the whole system itself can begin to “drift”. Aerospace engineers have been attempting to counteract this problem for years, but so far with little success. But a new paper from a research team led by Xia Lin and Baojun Lin at the Shanghai Engineering Center for Microsatellites, and published in Satellite Navigation, explains a potential answer to this problem - link the GPS satellites up with some in orbit around the Moon.

### A Buried Volcano on the Moon Hints At An Ancient Lunar Dynamo
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-06 10:12 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/a-buried-volcano-on-the-moon-hints-at-an-ancient-lunar-dynamo
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Scientists know for certain the moon doesn’t currently have a magnetosphere. But they’ve been debating for decades about whether it ever did. Data from both Apollo samples and orbital scientific instruments have proven divisive, and so the debate still rages. But a new paper from researchers at ETH Zurich, DLR, and the Institute of Applied Geosciences hopes to resolve the debate once and for all - by definitively proving that the Moon did, indeed, once have a magnetic dynamo.

### Space Radiation Sparks A Biological Cascade Of Cancer
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-05 11:08 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/space-radiation-sparks-a-biological-cascade-of-cancer
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Deep space travel comes with a huge potential downside - radiation exposure that can kill an astronaut either quickly or slowly over time if not managed correctly. But what exactly does that mean, and is there any way to handle it other than sticking more and more protective layers between the squishy biological systems inside a spacecraft and the radiative void of deep space? A new paper from researchers at Oklahoma State University and the University of Texas Health Science Center hopes to answer both questions, and while the causes of radiation they point out are complex, they also offer some potential solutions as well.

Source: Phys.org Mollusks
Published: 2026-08-27 13:00 UTC
URL: https://phys.org/news/2026-08-ai-uncover-oyster-dna-linked.html
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

While a colleague traveled along the East Coast from Canada to the Gulf of Mexico collecting oysters, Northeastern University bioinformatics graduate student Megha Prasad worked away at her computer. She zeroed in on DNA, the molecule containing instructions for making proteins that shape the critters' traits. It also holds the key to their adaptation to the changing climate.

Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

This post Arthropods Lag Far Behind Other Animals in Conservation Assessment and Protection, Study Shows appeared first on Entomology Today - Brought to you by the Entomological Society of America . Most U.S. insects and arachnids remain unassessed for conservation risk, and even many known at-risk species lack legal protection. Only 2.5% of at-risk insects and arachnids have nationwide protection under the U.S. Endangered Species Act, compared to 27.7% of at-risk birds, for instance. A study quantifying this lag in arthropod conservation reveals a major blind spot in efforts to save vulnerable wildlife. [Read more] The post Arthropods Lag Far Behind Other Animals in Conservation Assessment and Protection, Study Shows appeared first on Entomology Today .

I like writing Javascript without a build system and for the millionth time yesterday I ran into a problem where I needed to figure out how to import a Javascript library in my code without using a build system, and it took FOREVER to figure out how to import it because the library’s setup instructions assume that you’re using a build system. Luckily at this point I’ve mostly learned how to navigate this situation and either successfully use the library or decide it’s too difficult and switch to a different library, so here’s the guide I wish I had to importing Javascript libraries years ago.
